## [SCHEDULE 3]{.scheduleLabel}[(Subparagraphs 5(1)(a)(i) and (4)(a)(i), subsection 7(2), paragraph 7(7)(a) and Schedules 4 and 5)]{.OriginatingRef}[Value of Goods]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-1255935 .scheduleLabel}
:::

- **1** Unless otherwise stated, the following definitions apply in this
  Schedule.

  [[buyer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[buyer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} refers to a person who purchases a
      good from the producer. ([acheteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[buying commissions]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[buying commissions]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means fees paid by a
      buyer to that buyer's agent or mandatary for the agent or
      mandatary's services in representing the buyer in the purchase of
      a good. ([commission d'achat]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[producer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[producer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} refers to the producer of the
      good being valued. ([producteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- **2** For the purposes of subsection 7(2) of these Regulations, the
  transaction value of a good is the price actually paid or payable for
  the good, determined in accordance with section 3 and adjusted in
  accordance with section 4.

- - **3** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The price actually paid or payable is the
    total payment made or to be made by the buyer to or for the benefit
    of the producer. The payment need not necessarily take the form of a
    transfer of money; it may be made by letters of credit or negotiable
    instruments. The payment may be made directly or indirectly to the
    producer. For an illustration of this, the settlement by the buyer,
    whether in whole or in part, of a debt owed by the producer is an
    indirect payment.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} Activities undertaken by the buyer on the buyer's
    own account, other than those for which an adjustment is provided in
    section 4, are not considered to be an indirect payment, even though
    the activities may be regarded as being for the benefit of the
    producer. For an illustration of this, if the buyer, by agreement
    with the producer, undertakes activities relating to the marketing
    of the good, the costs of such activities are not to be added to the
    price actually paid or payable.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} The transaction value is not to include the
    following charges or costs, if they are distinguished from the price
    actually paid or payable:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} charges for construction, erection, assembly,
      maintenance or technical assistance related to the good undertaken
      after the good is sold to the buyer; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} duties and taxes paid in the country in which the
      buyer is located with respect to the good.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} The flow of dividends or other payments from the
    buyer to the producer that do not relate to the purchase of the good
    are not part of the transaction value.

- - **4** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In determining the transaction value of a
    good, the following must be added to the price actually paid or
    payable:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to the extent that they are incurred by the
      buyer, or by a related person on behalf of the buyer, with respect
      to the good being valued and are not included in the price
      actually paid or payable

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} commissions and brokerage fees, except buying
        commissions,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the costs of transporting the good to the
        producer's point of direct shipment and the costs of loading,
        unloading, handling and insurance that are associated with that
        transportation, and

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} if the packaging materials and containers are
        classified with the good under the Harmonized System, the value
        of the packaging materials and containers;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the value, reasonably allocated in accordance
      with subsection (13), of the following elements if they are
      supplied directly or indirectly to the producer by the buyer, free
      of charge or at reduced cost for use in connection with the
      production and sale of the good, to the extent that the value is
      not included in the price actually paid or payable:

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} materials, other than indirect materials, used
        in the production of the good,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} tools, dies, moulds and similar indirect
        materials used in the production of the good,

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} indirect materials, other than any of the
        indirect materials referred to in subparagraph (ii) or in
        paragraph (c), (e) or (f) of the definition [[indirect
        material]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 1(1) of these
        Regulations, used in the production of the good, and

      - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} engineering, development, artwork, design
        work, and plans and sketches necessary for the production of the
        good, regardless of where performed;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the royalties related to the good, other than
      charges with respect to the right to reproduce the good in the
      territory of one or more of the CUSMA countries, that the buyer
      must pay directly or indirectly as a condition of sale of the
      good, to the extent that such royalties are not included in the
      price actually paid or payable; and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the value of any part of the proceeds of any
      subsequent resale, disposal or use of the good that accrues
      directly or indirectly to the producer.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} The additions referred to in subsection (1) must be
    made to the price actually paid or payable under this section only
    on the basis of objective and quantifiable data.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} If objective and quantifiable data do not exist
    with regard to the additions required to be made to the price
    actually paid or payable under subsection (1), the transaction value
    cannot be determined under section 2.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} Additions must not be made to the price actually
    paid or payable for the purpose of determining the transaction value
    except as provided in this section.

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} The amounts to be added under subparagraphs
    (1)(a)(i) and (ii) are

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} those amounts that are recorded on the books of
      the buyer; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if those amounts are costs incurred by a related
      person on behalf of the buyer and are not recorded on the books of
      the buyer, those amounts that are recorded on the books of that
      related person.

  - [(6)]{.lawlabel} The value of the packaging materials and containers
    referred to in subparagraph (1)(a)(iii) and the value of the
    elements referred to in subparagraph (1)(b)(i) are

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the packaging materials and containers or the
      elements are imported from outside the territory of the CUSMA
      country in which the producer is located, the customs value of the
      packaging materials and containers or the elements;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the buyer, or a related person on behalf of
      the buyer, purchases the packaging materials and containers or the
      elements from a person in the territory of the CUSMA country in
      which the producer is located who is not a related person, the
      price actually paid or payable for the packaging materials and
      containers or the elements;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the buyer, or a related person on behalf of
      the buyer, acquires the packaging materials and containers or the
      elements from a person in the territory of the CUSMA country in
      which the producer is located who is not a related person other
      than through a purchase, the value of the consideration related to
      the acquisition of the packaging materials and containers or the
      elements, based on the cost of the consideration that is recorded
      on the books of the buyer or the related person; or

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the packaging materials and containers or the
      elements are produced by the buyer, or by a related person, in the
      territory of the CUSMA country in which the producer is located,
      the total cost of the packaging materials and containers or the
      elements, determined in accordance with subsection (8).

  - [(7)]{.lawlabel} The value referred to in subsection (6), to the
    extent that such costs are not included under paragraphs (6)(a) to
    (d), must include the following costs that are recorded on the books
    of the buyer or the related person supplying the packaging materials
    and containers or the elements on behalf of the buyer:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the costs of freight, insurance and packing, and
      all other costs incurred in transporting the packaging materials
      and containers or the elements to the location of the producer;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} duties and taxes paid or payable with respect to
      the packaging materials and containers or the elements, other than
      duties and taxes that are waived, refunded, refundable or
      otherwise recoverable, including credit against duty or tax paid
      or payable;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} customs brokerage fees, including the cost of
      in-house customs brokerage services, incurred with respect to the
      packaging materials and containers or the elements; and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the cost of waste and spoilage resulting from the
      use of the packaging materials and containers or the elements in
      the production of the good, less the value of renewable scrap or
      by-product.

  - [(8)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (6)(d), the total
    cost of the packaging materials and containers referred to in
    subparagraph (1)(a)(iii) or the elements referred to in subparagraph
    (1)(b)(i) is

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the packaging materials and containers or the
      elements are produced by the buyer, at the choice of the buyer,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the total cost incurred with respect to all
        goods produced by the buyer, calculated on the basis of the
        costs that are recorded on the books of the buyer, that can be
        reasonably allocated to the packaging materials and containers
        or the elements in accordance with Schedule 5, or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the aggregate of each cost incurred by the
        buyer that forms part of the total cost incurred with respect to
        the packaging materials and containers or the elements,
        calculated on the basis of the costs that are recorded on the
        books of the buyer, that can be reasonably allocated to the
        packaging materials and containers or the elements in accordance
        with Schedule 5; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the packaging materials and containers or the
      elements are produced by a person who is related to the buyer, at
      the choice of the buyer,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the total cost incurred with respect to all
        goods produced by that related person, calculated on the basis
        of the costs that are recorded on the books of that person, that
        can be reasonably allocated to the packaging materials and
        containers or the elements in accordance with Schedule 5, or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the aggregate of each cost incurred by that
        related person that forms part of the total cost incurred with
        respect to the packaging materials and containers or the
        elements, calculated on the basis of the costs that are recorded
        on the books of that person, that can be reasonably allocated to
        the packaging materials and containers or the elements in
        accordance with Schedule 5.

  - [(9)]{.lawlabel} Except as otherwise provided in subsections (11)
    and (12), the value of the elements referred to in subparagraphs
    (1)(b)(ii) to (iv) is

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the cost of those elements that is recorded on
      the books of the buyer; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if such elements are provided by another person
      on behalf of the buyer and the cost is not recorded on the books
      of the buyer, the cost of those elements that is recorded on the
      books of that other person.

  - [(10)]{.lawlabel} If the elements referred to in subparagraphs
    (1)(b)(ii) to (iv) were previously used by or on behalf of the
    buyer, the value of those elements must be adjusted downward to
    reflect that use.

  - [(11)]{.lawlabel} If the elements referred to in subparagraphs
    (1)(b)(ii) and (iii) were leased by the buyer or a person related to
    the buyer, the value of those elements are the cost of the lease as
    recorded on the books of the buyer or that related person.

  - [(12)]{.lawlabel} An addition must not be made to the price actually
    paid or payable for the elements referred to in subparagraph
    (1)(b)(iv) that are available in the public domain, other than the
    cost of obtaining copies of them.

  - [(13)]{.lawlabel} The producer must choose the method of allocating
    to the good the value of the elements referred to in subparagraphs
    (1)(b)(ii) to (iv), if the value is reasonably allocated to the
    good. The methods the producer may choose to allocate the value
    include allocating the value over the number of units produced up to
    the time of the first shipment or allocating the value over the
    entire anticipated production if contracts or firm commitments exist
    for that production. For an illustration of this, a buyer provides
    the producer with a mould to be used in the production of the good
    and contracts with the producer to buy 10,000 units of that good. By
    the time the first shipment of 1,000 units arrives, the producer has
    already produced 4,000 units. In these circumstances, the producer
    may choose to allocate the value of the mould over 4,000 units or
    10,000 units but must not choose to allocate the value of the
    elements to the first shipment of 1,000 units. The producer may
    choose to allocate the entire value of the elements to a single
    shipment of a good only if that single shipment comprises all of the
    units of the good acquired by the buyer under the contract or
    commitment for that number of units of the good between the producer
    and the buyer.

  - [(14)]{.lawlabel} The addition for the royalties referred to in
    paragraph (1)(c) is the payment for the royalties that is recorded
    on the books of the buyer or, if the payment for the royalties is
    recorded on the books of another person, the payment for the
    royalties that is recorded on the books of that other person.

  - [(15)]{.lawlabel} The value of the proceeds referred to in paragraph
    (1)(d) is the amount that is recorded for such proceeds on the books
    of the buyer or the producer.
::::

::::: section
:::: {#1255994 .Schedule}
::: {}
## [SCHEDULE 4]{.scheduleLabel}[(Subsection 8(4))]{.OriginatingRef}[Unacceptable Transaction Value]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-1255995 .scheduleLabel}
:::

- **1** Unless otherwise stated, the following definitions apply in this
  Schedule.

  [[buyer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[buyer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} refers to a person who purchases a
      good from the producer. ([acheteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[producer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[producer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} refers to the producer of the
      good being valued. ([producteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- - **2** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There is no transaction value for a good if
    the good is not the subject of a sale.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} The transaction value of a good is unacceptable if

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there are restrictions on the disposal or use of
      the good by the buyer, other than restrictions that

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} are imposed or required by law or by the public
        authorities in the territory of the CUSMA country in which the
        buyer is located,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} limit the geographical area in which the good
        may be resold, or

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} do not substantially affect the value of the
        good;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the sale or price actually paid or payable is
      subject to a condition or consideration for which a value cannot
      be determined with respect to the good;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} part of the proceeds of any subsequent resale,
      disposal or use of the good by the buyer will accrue directly or
      indirectly to the producer and an appropriate addition to the
      price actually paid or payable cannot be made in accordance with
      paragraph 4(1)(d) of Schedule 3; or

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the producer and the buyer are related persons
      and the relationship between them influenced the price actually
      paid or payable for the good.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} The conditions or considerations referred to in
    paragraph (2)(b) include the following circumstances:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the producer establishes the price actually paid
      or payable for the good on condition that the buyer will also buy
      other goods in specified quantities;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the price actually paid or payable for the good
      is dependent on the price or prices at which the buyer sells other
      goods to the producer; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the price actually paid or payable is established
      on the basis of a form of payment extraneous to the good, such as
      when the good is a semi-finished good that is provided by the
      producer to the buyer on condition that the producer will receive
      a specified quantity of the finished good from the buyer.

  - [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (2)(b), conditions or
    considerations relating to the production or marketing of the good
    do not render the transaction value unacceptable, such as if the
    buyer undertakes on the buyer's own account, even though by
    agreement with the producer, activities relating to the marketing of
    the good.

  - [(5)]{.lawlabel} If objective and quantifiable data do not exist
    with regard to the additions required to be made to the price
    actually paid or payable under subsection 4(1) of Schedule 3, the
    transaction value cannot be determined under section 2 of that
    Schedule. For an illustration of this, a royalty is paid on the
    basis of the price actually paid or payable in a sale of a litre of
    a particular good that was purchased by the kilogram and made up
    into a solution. If the royalty is based partially on the purchased
    good and partially on other factors that have nothing to do with
    that good, such as when the purchased good is mixed with other
    ingredients and is no longer separately identifiable, or when the
    royalty cannot be distinguished from special financial arrangements
    between the producer and the buyer, it would be inappropriate to add
    the royalty and the transaction value of the good could not be
    determined. However, if the amount of the royalty is based only on
    the purchased good and can be readily quantified, an addition to the
    price actually paid or payable can be made and the transaction value
    can be determined.
::::
:::::

::::: section
:::: {#1256016 .Schedule}
::: {}
## [SCHEDULE 5]{.scheduleLabel}[(Subsections 5(11), 7(11) and (22) and 8(8) and Schedules 3 and 6)]{.OriginatingRef}[Reasonable Allocation of Costs]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-1256017 .scheduleLabel}
:::

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#interpretation .SchedHeadL2}

- **1** The following definitions apply in this Schedule.

  [[costs]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[costs]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any costs that are included in
      total cost and that can or must be allocated in a reasonable
      manner under subsections 5(11), 7(11) and 8(8) of these
      Regulations, subsection 4(8) of Schedule 3 and subsections 4(8)
      and 9(3) of Schedule 6. ([coûts]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[discontinued operation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[discontinued operation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in the case of a
      producer located in a CUSMA country, has the meaning set out in
      that CUSMA country's Generally Accepted Accounting
      Principles. ([activité abandonnée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[indirect overhead]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[indirect overhead]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means period costs and
      other costs. ([frais généraux indirects]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[internal management purpose]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[internal management purpose]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any
      purpose relating to tax reporting, financial reporting, financial
      planning, decision making, pricing, cost recovery, cost control
      management or performance measurement. ([fins de gestion
      interne]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[overhead]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[overhead]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means costs, other than direct
      material costs and direct labour costs. ([frais
      généraux]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- - **2** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this Schedule, a reference to "producer",
    for the purposes of subsection 4(8) of Schedule 3, is to be read as
    a reference to "buyer".

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} In this Schedule, a reference to "good"

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of subsection 7(15) of these
      Regulations, is to be read as a reference to "identical goods or
      similar goods, or any combination thereof";

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of subsection 8(8) of these
      Regulations, is to be read as a reference to "intermediate
      material";

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of section 16 of these
      Regulations, is to be read as a reference to "category of vehicles
      that is chosen pursuant to subsection 16(1) of these Regulations";

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of subsection 4(8) of Schedule
      3, is to be read as a reference to "packaging materials and
      containers or the elements"; and

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of subsection 4(8) of Schedule
      6, is to be read as a reference to "elements".

#### [Methods to Reasonably Allocate Costs]{.HTitleText2} {#methods-to-reasonably-allocate-costs .SchedHeadL2}

- - **3** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a producer of a good is using, for an
    internal management purpose, a cost allocation method to allocate to
    the good direct material costs, or part thereof, and that method
    reasonably reflects the direct material used in the production of
    the good based on the criterion of benefit, cause or ability to
    bear, that method must be used to reasonably allocate the costs to
    the good.

  - [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a producer of a good is using, for an internal
    management purpose, a cost allocation method to allocate to the good
    direct labour costs, or part thereof, and that method reasonably
    reflects the direct labour used in the production of the good based
    on the criterion of benefit, cause or ability to bear, that method
    must be used to reasonably allocate the costs to the good.

  - [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a producer of a good is using, for an internal
    management purpose, a cost allocation method to allocate to the good
    overhead, or part thereof, and that method is based on the criterion
    of benefit, cause or ability to bear, that method must be used to
    reasonably allocate the costs to the good.

- **4** If costs are not reasonably allocated to a good under section 3,
  those costs are reasonably allocated to the good if they are allocated

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} with respect to direct material costs, on the basis
    of any method that reasonably reflects the direct material used in
    the production of the good based on the criterion of benefit, cause
    or ability to bear;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} with respect to direct labour costs, on the basis
    of any method that reasonably reflects the direct labour used in the
    production of the good based on the criterion of benefit, cause or
    ability to bear; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} with respect to overhead, on the basis of any of
    the following methods:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the method set out in Appendix A, B or C,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a method based on a combination of the methods
      set out in Appendices A and B or Appendices A and C, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a cost allocation method based on the criterion
      of benefit, cause or ability to bear.

- **5** Notwithstanding sections 3 and 8, if a producer allocates, for
  an internal management purpose, costs to a good that is not produced
  in the period in which the costs are expensed on the books of the
  producer (such as costs with respect to research and development and
  obsolete materials), those costs are considered reasonably allocated
  if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of subsection 7(11) of these
    Regulations, they are allocated to a good that is produced in the
    period in which the costs are expensed; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the good produced in that period is within a group
    or range of goods, including identical goods or similar goods, that
    is produced by the same industry or industry sector as the goods to
    which the costs are expensed.

- **6** Any cost allocation method referred to in section 3, 4 or 5 that
  is used by a producer for the purposes of these Regulations must be
  used throughout the producer's fiscal year.

#### [Costs Not Reasonably Allocated]{.HTitleText2} {#costs-not-reasonably-allocated .SchedHeadL2}

- **7** The allocation to a good of any of the following is considered
  not to be reasonably allocated to the good:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} costs of a service provided by a producer of a good
    to another person if the service is not related to the good;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} gains or losses resulting from the disposal of a
    discontinued operation, except gains or losses related to the
    production of the good;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} cumulative effects of accounting changes reported
    in accordance with a specific requirement of the applicable
    Generally Accepted Accounting Principles; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} gains or losses resulting from the sale of a
    capital asset of the producer.

- **8** Any costs allocated under section 3 on the basis of a cost
  allocation method that is used for an internal management purpose
  solely to qualify a good as an originating good are considered not to
  be reasonably allocated.
::::
:::::

::::::: section
:::::: {#1256057 .Schedule}
::: {}
## [APPENDIX A]{.scheduleLabel}[Cost Ratio Method]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-1256058 .scheduleLabel}
:::

#### [Calculation of Cost Ratio]{.HTitleText2} {#calculation-of-cost-ratio .SchedHeadL2}

For the overhead to be allocated, the producer may choose one or more
allocation bases that reflect a relationship between the overhead and
the good based on the criterion of benefit, cause or ability to bear.

With respect to each allocation base that is chosen by the producer for
allocating overhead, a cost ratio is calculated for each good produced
by the producer as determined by the formula

::: {#1256062 .Provision}
CR = AB ÷ TAB

where

[CR]{.dfn}
:   is the cost ratio with respect to the good;

[AB]{.dfn}
:   is the allocation base for the good; and

[TAB]{.dfn}
:   is the total allocation base for all the goods produced by the
    producer.
:::

#### [Allocation to a Good of Costs Included in Overhead]{.HTitleText2} {#allocation-to-a-good-of-costs-included-in-overhead .SchedHeadL2}

The costs with respect to which an allocation base is chosen are
allocated to a good in accordance with the following formula:

::: {#1256069 .Provision}
CAG = CA × CR

where

[CAG]{.dfn}
:   is the costs allocated to the good;

[CA]{.dfn}
:   is the costs to be allocated; and

[CR]{.dfn}
:   is the cost ratio with respect to the good.
:::

#### [Excluded Costs]{.HTitleText2} {#excluded-costs .SchedHeadL2}

Under paragraph 7(11)(b) of these Regulations, if excluded costs are
included in costs to be allocated to a good, the cost ratio used to
allocate that cost to the good is used to determine the amount of
excluded costs to be subtracted from the costs allocated to the good.

#### [Allocation Bases for Costs]{.HTitleText2} {#allocation-bases-for-costs .SchedHeadL2}

The following is a non-exhaustive list of allocation bases that may be
used by the producer to calculate cost ratios:

<figure>
<ul>
<li><div class="listItemBlock1">
<div class="listItemLabel">

</div>
<div class="listItemText0">
Direct labour hours
</div>
</div></li>
<li><div class="listItemBlock1">
<div class="listItemLabel">

</div>
<div class="listItemText0">
Direct labour costs
</div>
</div></li>
<li><div class="listItemBlock1">
<div class="listItemLabel">

</div>
<div class="listItemText0">
Units produced
</div>
</div></li>
<li><div class="listItemBlock1">
<div class="listItemLabel">

</div>
<div class="listItemText0">
Machine-hours
</div>
</div></li>
<li><div class="listItemBlock1">
<div class="listItemLabel">

</div>
<div class="listItemText0">
Sales dollars or pesos
</div>
</div></li>
<li><div class="listItemBlock1">
<div class="listItemLabel">

</div>
<div class="listItemText0">
Floor space
</div>
</div></li>
</ul>
</figure>

#### [Examples]{.HTitleText2} {#examples .SchedHeadL2}

*The following examples illustrate the application of the cost ratio
method to costs included in overhead.*

##### [Example 1: Direct Labour Hours]{.HTitleText3} {#example-1-direct-labour-hours .SchedHeadL3}

*A producer who produces Good A and Good B may allocate overhead on the
basis of direct labour hours spent to produce Good A and Good B. A total
of 8,000 direct labour hours have been spent to produce Good A and Good
B: 5,000 hours with respect to Good A and 3,000 hours with respect to
Good B. The amount of overhead to be allocated is \$6,000,000.*

*Calculation of the ratios*:

*Good A: 5,000 hours ÷ 8,000 hours = 0.625*

*Good B: 3,000 hours ÷ 8,000 hours = 0.375*

*Allocation of overhead to Good A and Good B*:

*Good A: \$6,000,000 × 0.625 = \$3,750,000*

*Good B: \$6,000,000 × 0.375 = \$2,250,000*

##### [Example 2: Direct Labour Costs]{.HTitleText3} {#example-2-direct-labour-costs .SchedHeadL3}

*A producer who produces Good A and Good B may allocate overhead on the
basis of direct labour costs incurred in the production of Good A and
Good B. The total direct labour costs incurred in the production of Good
A and Good B is \$60,000: \$50,000 with respect to Good A and \$10,000
with respect to Good B. The amount of overhead to be allocated is
\$6,000,000.*

*Calculation of the ratios*:

*Good A: \$50,000 ÷ \$60,000 = 0.833*

*Good B: \$10,000 ÷ \$60,000 = 0.167*

*Allocation of Overhead to Good A and Good B*:

*Good A: \$6,000,000 × 0.833 = \$4,998,000*

*Good B: \$6,000,000 × 0.167 = \$1,002,000*

##### [Example 3: Units Produced]{.HTitleText3} {#example-3-units-produced .SchedHeadL3}

*A producer of Good A and Good B may allocate overhead on the basis of
units produced. The total units of Good A and Good B produced is
150,000: 100,000 units of Good A and 50,000 units of Good B. The amount
of overhead to be allocated is \$6,000,000.*

*Calculation of the ratios*:

*Good A: 100,000 units ÷ 150,000 units = 0.667*

*Good B: 50,000 units ÷ 150,000 units = 0.333*

*Allocation of overhead to Good A and Good B*:

*Good A: \$6,000,000 × 0.667 = \$4,002,000*

*Good B: \$6,000,000 × 0.333 = \$1,998,000*

##### [Example 4: Machine-hours]{.HTitleText3} {#example-4-machine-hours .SchedHeadL3}

*A producer who produces Good A and Good B may allocate machine-related
overhead on the basis of machine-hours utilized in the production of
Good A and Good B. The total machine-hours utilized for the production
of Good A and Good B is 3,000 hours: 1,200 hours with respect to Good A
and 1,800 hours with respect to Good B. The amount of machine-related
overhead to be allocated is \$6,000,000.*

*Calculation of the ratios*:

*Good A: 1,200 machine-hours ÷ 3,000 machine-hours = 0.40*

*Good B: 1,800 machine-hours ÷ 3,000 machine-hours = 0.60*

*Allocation of machine-related overhead to Good A and Good B*:

*Good A: \$6,000,000 × 0.40 = \$2,400,000*

*Good B: \$6,000,000 × 0.60 = \$3,600,000*

##### [Example 5: Sales Dollars or Pesos]{.HTitleText3} {#example-5-sales-dollars-or-pesos .SchedHeadL3}

*A producer who produces Good A and Good B may allocate overhead on the
basis of sales dollars. The producer sold 2,000 units of Good A at
\$4,000 per unit and 200 units of Good B at \$3,000 per unit. The amount
of overhead to be allocated is \$6,000,000.*

*Total sales dollars for Good A and Good B*:

*Good A: \$4,000 × 2,000 units = \$8,000,000*

*Good B: \$3,000 × 200 units = \$600,000*

Total sales dollars: \$8,000,000 + \$600,000 = \$8,600,000

*Calculation of the ratios*:

*Good A: \$8,000,000 ÷ \$8,600,000 = 0.93*

*Good B: \$600,000 ÷ \$8,600,000 = 0.07*

*Allocation of overhead to Good A and Good B*:

*Good A: \$6,000,000 × 0.93 = \$5,580,000*

*Good B: \$6,000,000 × 0.07 = \$420,000*

##### [Example 6: Floor Space]{.HTitleText3} {#example-6-floor-space .SchedHeadL3}

*A producer who produces Good A and Good B may allocate overhead
relating to utilities (heat, water and electricity) on the basis of
floor space used in the production and storage of Good A and Good B. The
total floor space used in the production and storage of Good A and Good
B is 100,000 ft^2^: 40,000 ft^2^ with respect to Good A and 60,000 ft^2^
with respect to Good B. The amount of overhead to be allocated is
\$6,000,000.*

*Calculation of the ratios*:

*Good A: 40,000 ft^2^ ÷ 100,000 ft^2^ = 0.40*

*Good B: 60,000 ft^2^ ÷ 100,000 ft^2^ = 0.60*

*Allocation of overhead (utilities) to Good A and Good B*:

*Good A: \$6,000,000 × 0.40 = \$2,400,000*

*Good B: \$6,000,000 × 0.60 = \$3,600,000*
::::::
:::::::

::::::: section
:::::: {#1256139 .Schedule}
::: {}
## [APPENDIX B]{.scheduleLabel}[Direct Labour and Direct Material Ratio Method]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-1256140 .scheduleLabel}
:::

#### [Calculation of Direct Labour and Direct Material Ratio]{.HTitleText2} {#calculation-of-direct-labour-and-direct-material-ratio .SchedHeadL2}

For each good produced by the producer, a direct labour and direct
material ratio is calculated by the formula

::: {#1256143 .Provision}
DLDMR = (DLC + DMC) ÷ (TDLC + TDMC)

where

[DLDMR]{.dfn}
:   is the direct labour and direct material ratio for the good;

[DLC]{.dfn}
:   is the direct labour costs of the good;

[DMC]{.dfn}
:   is the direct material costs of the good;

[TDLC]{.dfn}
:   is the total direct labour costs of all goods produced by the
    producer; and

[TDMC]{.dfn}
:   is the total direct material costs of all goods produced by the
    producer.
:::

#### [Allocation of Overhead to a Good]{.HTitleText2} {#allocation-of-overhead-to-a-good .SchedHeadL2}

Overhead is allocated to a good as determined by the formula

::: {#1256152 .Provision}
OAG = O × DLDMR

where

[OAG]{.dfn}
:   is the overhead allocated to the good;

[O]{.dfn}
:   is the overhead to be allocated; and

[DLDMR]{.dfn}
:   is the direct labour and direct material ratio for the good.
:::

#### [Excluded Costs]{.HTitleText2} {#excluded-costs-1 .SchedHeadL2}

Under paragraph 7(11)(b) of these Regulations, if excluded costs are
included in overhead to be allocated to a good, the direct labour and
direct material ratio used to allocate overhead to the good is used to
determine the amount of excluded costs to be subtracted from the
overhead allocated to the good.

#### [Examples]{.HTitleText2} {#examples-1 .SchedHeadL2}

##### [Example 1]{.HTitleText3} {#example-1 .SchedHeadL3}

*The following example illustrates the application of the direct labour
and direct material ratio method used by a producer of a good to
allocate overhead if the producer chooses to calculate the net cost of
the good in accordance with paragraph 7(11)(a) of these Regulations.*

*A producer produces Good A and Good B. Overhead (O) minus excluded
costs (EC) is \$30 and the other relevant costs are set out in the
following table:*

<figure id="1256163">
<table class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize7 none">
<thead>
<tr>
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top"></th>
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col"><em>Good A
($)</em></th>
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col"><em>Good B
($)</em></th>
<th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col"><em>Total ($)</em></th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="row"><em>Direct
labour costs (DLC)</em></th>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top alignleft"><em>5</em></td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top alignleft"><em>5</em></td>
<td class="noBorderBottom top alignleft"><em>10</em></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="row"><em>Direct
material costs (DMC)</em></th>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top alignleft"><em><span
class="Underline">10</span></em></td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top alignleft"><em><span
class="Underline">5</span></em></td>
<td class="noBorderBottom top alignleft"><em><span
class="Underline">15</span></em></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<th class="noBorderRight top" scope="row"><em>Totals</em></th>
<td class="noBorderRight top alignleft"><em>15</em></td>
<td class="noBorderRight top alignleft"><em>10</em></td>
<td class="top alignleft"><em>25</em></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</figure>

*Overhead allocated to Good A*

*OAG (Good A) = O (\$30) × DLDMR (\$15 ÷ \$25)*

*OAG (Good A) = \$18.00*

*Overhead allocated to Good B*

*OAG (Good B) = O (\$30) × DLDMR (\$10 ÷ \$25)*

*OAG (Good B) = \$12.00*

##### [Example 2]{.HTitleText3} {#example-2 .SchedHeadL3}

*The following example illustrates the application of the direct labour
and direct material ratio method used by a producer of a good to
allocate overhead if the producer chooses to calculate the net cost of
the good in accordance with paragraph 7(11)(b) of these Regulations and
if excluded costs are included in overhead.*

*A producer produces Good A and Good B. Overhead (O) is \$50 (including
excluded costs (EC) of \$20). The other relevant costs are set out in
the table to Example 1.*

*Overhead allocated to Good A*

*OAG (Good A) = \[O (\$50) × DLDMR (\$15 ÷ \$25)\] - \[EC (\$20) × DLDMR
(\$15 ÷ \$25)\]*

*OAG (Good A) = \$18.00*

*Overhead allocated to Good B*

*OAG (Good B) = \[O (\$50) × DLDMR (\$10 ÷ \$25)\] − \[EC (\$20) × DLDMR
(\$10 ÷ \$25)\]*

*OAG (Good B) = \$12.00*
